A grown-up-sized long-sleeved V-neck T-shirt in brushed flannel, sewn as a real fabric version of the outfit of a haunted marionette. The top is made as a puppet costume with strings, in wool-blend suiting with minky plush trims, every collar, lapel, strap and pocket a separate sewn piece. The garment runs down to a straight hem, finished with a sewn waistband, belt or sash band. The sewn garment has a standard T-shirt silhouette with a straight hem across the bottom. It is tailored like a real shirt, with proper seams, hems and stitching lines, entirely in the outfit's own colours and cloths. The uniform fills the whole straight-sided garment shape edge to edge, the same width at chest, waist and hem. A wearable shirt version of the outfit, made from sewn panels with real trims. The tee is clearly a costume tee, not a plain shirt, it shows the headwear's emblem or crest worked into the chest, the most recognisable props and accessories of the outfit as appliqué pieces and the belt, straps and buckles of the outfit, so anyone can tell at a glance what outfit it stands for.
A grown-up-sized long-sleeved V-neck T-shirt in quilted cotton, sewn as a real fabric version of the outfit of a trickster god. The top is made as a green and gold cape, in ribbed knit with satin trims, every collar, lapel, strap and pocket a separate sewn piece. The sleeve hems are cut at an angle, slanted, pointed or curved, to follow the outfit's own cuffs, and the print follows that edge exactly. The garment runs down to a straight hem, finished with a sewn waistband, belt or sash band. It is a T-shirt in shape, straight-sided, with set-in sleeves and a straight hem. Layered fabric pieces are sewn together and colour-blocked in the outfit's colours, with finished edges and neat stitching throughout. The outfit is stretched to the garment's own straight-sided shape and fills it completely, so it suits any body size. The T-shirt is the outfit itself, constructed from fabric pieces, comfortable and wearable as a shirt. To read as a costume rather than a plain top, it adds the lower garment's colours and pattern as a band across the hem, the collar, neckwear and fastenings of the outfit and its pockets, flaps and trims, bold and large enough to identify the outfit from across a room.

A hood at the collar is sewn as a straw boater. The shirt ends in a straight hem, with a sewn belt or sash at the waist where the outfit has one. The shirt keeps the shape of an ordinary T-shirt, with a round neckline, straight side seams, sleeves and a straight open hem. The shirt is assembled from cut panels joined with visible seams, topstitching and piping, hanging as one coherent piece in the outfit's own cloth. The uniform fills the whole straight-sided garment shape edge to edge, the same width at chest, waist and hem. The T-shirt is the outfit itself, constructed from fabric pieces, comfortable and wearable as a shirt. The tee is clearly a costume tee, not a plain shirt, it shows the belt, straps and buckles of the outfit, the held item or gear of the outfit, shown as a patch or large motif on the chest or a sleeve and its pockets, flaps and trims, so anyone can tell at a glance what outfit it stands for.
A grown-up-sized long-sleeved straight-cut T-shirt in chenille, sewn as a real fabric version of the outfit of a night vigilante. It is a charcoal hooded armoured jacket with a short cape made in cloth, sequin fabric panels with sherpa details, joined by visible seams and finished with real stitched trims. The sleeve hems are cut at an angle, slanted, pointed or curved, to follow the outfit's own cuffs, and the print follows that edge exactly. The garment runs down to a straight hem, finished with a sewn waistband, belt or sash band. The sewn garment has a standard T-shirt silhouette with a straight hem across the bottom. The shirt is assembled from cut panels joined with visible seams, topstitching and piping, hanging as one coherent piece in the outfit's own cloth. The outfit is laid out on the garment's own rectangle shape, fully covering it from side seam to side seam. It is a cut-and-sew costume T-shirt, a real garment sewn to look like the outfit. It carries enough of the costume to be unmistakable, the footwear's trim colours as stripes on the cuffs and hem, the belt, straps and buckles of the outfit and the outfit's badges, insignia and emblems, all in the outfit's own colours.

The sleeve hems are cut at an angle, slanted, pointed or curved, to follow the outfit's own cuffs, and the print follows that edge exactly. The sewn garment has a standard T-shirt silhouette with a straight hem across the bottom. The outfit is laid out on the garment's own rectangle shape, fully covering it from side seam to side seam. The T-shirt is the outfit itself, constructed from fabric pieces, comfortable and wearable as a shirt. The uniform fills the whole straight-sided garment shape edge to edge, the same width at chest, waist and hem. It is a cut-and-sew costume T-shirt, a real garment sewn to look like the outfit. The tee is clearly a costume tee, not a plain shirt, it shows the belt, straps and buckles of the outfit, the collar, neckwear and fastenings of the outfit and the headwear's emblem or crest worked into the chest, so anyone can tell at a glance what outfit it stands for.

The shirt is assembled from cut panels joined with visible seams, topstitching and piping, hanging as one coherent piece in the outfit's own cloth. The outfit is stretched to the garment's own straight-sided shape and fills it completely, so it suits any body size. It is a cut-and-sew costume T-shirt, a real garment sewn to look like the outfit. The tee is clearly a costume tee, not a plain shirt, it shows its pockets, flaps and trims, the footwear's trim colours as stripes on the cuffs and hem and the headwear's emblem or crest worked into the chest, so anyone can tell at a glance what outfit it stands for.

The garment runs down to a straight hem, finished with a sewn waistband, belt or sash band. It is a T-shirt in shape, straight-sided, with set-in sleeves and a straight hem. The shirt is assembled from cut panels joined with visible seams, topstitching and piping, hanging as one coherent piece in the outfit's own cloth. The outfit is stretched to the garment's own straight-sided shape and fills it completely, so it suits any body size. It is a cut-and-sew costume T-shirt, a real garment sewn to look like the outfit. The tee is clearly a costume tee, not a plain shirt, it shows the footwear's trim colours as stripes on the cuffs and hem, the collar, neckwear and fastenings of the outfit and the belt, straps and buckles of the outfit, so anyone can tell at a glance what outfit it stands for.

It carries enough of the costume to be unmistakable, the footwear's trim colours as stripes on the cuffs and hem, the lower garment's colours and pattern as a band across the hem and the outfit's badges, insignia and emblems, all in the outfit's own colours.

The shirt ends in a straight hem, with a sewn belt or sash at the waist where the outfit has one. It is a T-shirt in shape, straight-sided, with set-in sleeves and a straight hem. Layered fabric pieces are sewn together and colour-blocked in the outfit's colours, with finished edges and neat stitching throughout. The outfit is laid out on the garment's own rectangle shape, fully covering it from side seam to side seam. The T-shirt is the outfit itself, constructed from fabric pieces, comfortable and wearable as a shirt. It carries enough of the costume to be unmistakable, the footwear's trim colours as stripes on the cuffs and hem, the belt, straps and buckles of the outfit and the most recognisable props and accessories of the outfit as appliqué pieces, all in the outfit's own colours.
